=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
This problem is a harder version of Problem C. Here, the sequence is split into three subarrays.

You are given an integer sequence of length N: A = (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N).
When splitting A at two positions into three non-empty (contiguous) subarrays, find the maximum possible sum of the counts of distinct integers in those subarrays.
More formally, find the maximum sum of the following three values for a pair of integers (i,j) such that 1 \leq i < j \leq N-1: the count of distinct integers in (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_i), the count of distinct integers in (A_{i+1},A_{i+2},\ldots,A_j), and the count of distinct integers in (A_{j+1},A_{j+2},\ldots,A_{N}).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 3 \leq N \leq 3 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq N (1 \leq i \leq N)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5
3 1 4 1 5

Sample Output 1

5

If we let (i,j) = (2,4) to split the sequence into three subarrays (3,1), (4,1), (5), the counts of distinct integers in those subarrays are 2, 2, 1, respectively, for a total of 5. This sum cannot be greater than 5, so the answer is 5. Other partitions, such as (i,j) = (1,3), (2,3), (3,4), also achieve this sum.

Sample Input 2

10
2 5 6 4 4 1 1 3 1 4

Sample Output 2

9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's design iterative lazy segment tree for range add and range max.

Option 1: Use iterative segment tree with lazy propagation from AtCoder Library:
- size = 1 << ceil_pow2(n)
- d = [-INF]*(2*size); lz=[0]*size
- set leaves d[size+i]=f[i+1]? Need 0-index.
- build for i size-1..1: d[i]=max(d[2i],d[2i+1])
- all_apply(k, f): d[k]+=f; if k<size: lz[k]+=f
- push(k): if lz[k]!=0: all_apply(2k,lz[k]); all_apply(2k+1,lz[k]); lz[k]=0
- range_apply(l,r,f) half-open [l,r): 
    l += size; r += size
    l0=l; r0=r
    for i in range(log,0,-1): if ((l0>>i)<<i)!=l0: push(l0>>i); if ((r0>>i)<<i)!=r0: push((r0-1)>>i)
    while l<r: if l&1: all_apply(l,f); l+=1; if r&1: r-=1; all_apply(r,f); l>>=1; r>>=1
    for i in range(1,log+1): if ((l0>>i)<<i)!=l0: update(l0>>i); if ((r0>>i)<<i)!=r0: update((r0-1)>>i)
- prod(l,r) similar with push then accumulate max.

This is standard but lengthy. Could also use recursive segment tree maybe simpler and accepted. Need decide.

Now, segment tree implementation. Let's write an iterative lazy segment tree class or functions.

AtCoder LazySegTree for range add/range max:

class LazySegTree:
    def __init__(self, v):
        self.n = len(v)
        self.size = 1
        self.log = 0
        while self.size < self.n:
            self.size <<= 1
            self.log += 1
        self.d = [-10**18]*(2*self.size)
        self.lz = [0]*self.size
        for i in range(self.n):
            self.d[self.size+i] = v[i]
        for i in range(self.size-1,0,-1):
            self.d[i] = max(self.d[2*i], self.d[2*i+1])
    def all_apply(self,k,f):
        self.d[k] += f
        if k < self.size:
            self.lz[k] += f
    def push(self,k):
        z = self.lz[k]
        if z:
            self.all_apply(2*k,z)
            self.all_apply(2*k+1,z)
            self.lz[k]=0
    def range_add(self,l,r,f): # [l,r)
        if l>=r: return
        l += self.size
        r += self.size
        l0=l; r0=r
        for i in range(self.log,0,-1):
            if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0:
                self.push(l0 >> i)
            if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0:
                self.push((r0-1) >> i)
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                self.all_apply(l,f)
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                self.all_apply(r,f)
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        l = l0; r = r0
        for i in range(1,self.log+1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                self.d[l >> i] = max(self.d[2*(l>>i)], self.d[2*(l>>i)+1])
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                self.d[(r-1) >> i] = max(self.d[2*((r-1)>>i)], self.d[2*((r-1)>>i)+1])
    def range_max(self,l,r):
        if l>=r: return -10**18
        l += self.size
        r += self.size
        for i in range(self.log,0,-1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                self.push(l >> i)
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                self.push((r-1) >> i)
        sml = -10**18
        smr = -10**18
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                sml = max(sml, self.d[l])
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                smr = max(self.d[r], smr)
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        return max(sml, smr)

Need ensure update rebuild conditions. Standard AtCoder:
for i in range(1, log+1):
    if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0: update(l0 >> i)
    if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0: update((r0-1) >> i)
This uses original l0,r0 before adding size? In AtCoder, l += size, r += size, l0=l, r0=r. Then after apply, update ancestors:

    if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0: d[l0 >> i] = op(d[2*(l0>>i)], d[2*(l0>>i)+1])
    if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0: d[(r0-1) >> i] = ...
Where l0,r0 are shifted (with size). Yes.

Need push in range_max before query. AtCoder prod:
for i in range(log,0,-1):
    if ((l >> i) << i) != l: push(l >> i)
    if ((r >> i) << i) != r: push((r-1) >> i)
Then accumulate. Good.

Let's craft efficient iterative lazy segment tree functions.

Pseudo:

```
size = 1
log = 0
while size < N:
    size <<= 1
    log += 1

d = [NEG] * (2*size)
lz = [0] * size

# fill leaves with f[i]
seen = [False]*(N+1)
cnt=0
for i in range(1,N+1):
    x=A[i]
    if not seen[x]:
        seen[x]=True
        cnt +=1
    d[size + i - 1] = cnt
for i in range(size-1,0,-1):
    d[i] = d[2*i] if d[2*i] >= d[2*i+1] else d[2*i+1]

def all_apply(k, f):
    d[k] += f
    if k < size:
        lz[k] += f

def push(k):
    z = lz[k]
    if z:
        d[2*k] += z
        if 2*k < size: lz[2*k] += z
        d[2*k+1] += z
        if 2*k+1 < size: lz[2*k+1] += z
        lz[k] = 0
```
But `all_apply` function call inside loops can be overhead. Inline in push and range_add.

`push(k)` called many times. We can define:
```
def push(k):
    z = lz[k]
    if z:
        lc = k<<1
        d[lc] += z
        if lc < size: lz[lc] += z
        rc = lc|1
        d[rc] += z
        if rc < size: lz[rc] += z
        lz[k] = 0
```
Need if k < size? push called with k<size always? In loops, push(l>>i) where l>>i can be 0? For i=log, l>>log could be 1 or maybe 0? l>=size, so l>>log >=1. r-1>>i >=1. So k>=1. Could be >=size? If i=0? loop starts log down to1, so k < size? For leaf? l>>1 < size? If l in [size,2size), l>>1 in [size/2,size), so <size. Good. So push only internal nodes. But if size=1? N>=3 so size>=4? Actually size power of two >=3 =>4. So okay. In all_apply, if k < size update lz. In range_add, all_apply can be called with k leaf or internal. Need if k < size. We'll inline:
```
if l & 1:
    d[l] += f
    if l < size: lz[l] += f
    l += 1
...
```
For `push`, z may be 0 often? Many nodes have lz. Fine.

`range_add`:
```
def range_add(l, r, val):
    if l >= r: return
    l += size
    r += size
    l0 = l
    r0 = r
    for i in range(log, 0, -1):
        if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0:
            push(l0 >> i)
        if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0:
            push((r0 - 1) >> i)
    while l < r:
        if l & 1:
            d[l] += val
            if l < size:
                lz[l] += val
            l += 1
        if r & 1:
            r -= 1
            d[r] += val
            if r < size:
                lz[r] += val
        l >>= 1
        r >>= 1
    l = l0
    r = r0
    for i in range(1, log + 1):
        if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
            k = l >> i
            d[k] = d[k<<1] if d[k<<1] >= d[k<<1|1] else d[k<<1|1]
        if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
            k = (r - 1) >> i
            d[k] = d[k<<1] if d[k<<1] >= d[k<<1|1] else d[k<<1|1]
```
Need update after all_apply: If both l and r conditions update same node? Standard handles; if same, second may recompute same. Fine.

`range_max`:
```
def range_max(l, r):
    if l >= r: return NEG
    l += size
    r += size
    for i in range(log, 0, -1):
        if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
            push(l >> i)
        if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
            push((r - 1) >> i)
    res = NEG
    while l < r:
        if l & 1:
            if d[l] > res: res = d[l]
            l += 1
        if r & 1:
            r -= 1
            if d[r] > res: res = d[r]
        l >>= 1
        r >>= 1
    return res
```
Need note in range_max, after push, d values updated. Good.

Could optimize push condition using `if (l0 >> i) << i != l0` standard. This condition checks if l0 is not aligned at a block of size 2^i. For l0=size (power of two) and i=log, aligned. For l0 not aligned, need push ancestors. Good.

Potential bug: In range_add, after applying, update ancestors using l0,r0. But if l0 or r0 conditions false, no update. Standard.

push loop i=2: l0>>2=1, ((5>>2)<<2)=4 !=5 push(1) root lz=0. r0>>2=1, ((6>>2)<<2)=4 !=6 push((5)>>2=1) root again. i=1: l0>>1=2, ((5>>1)<<1)=4 !=5 push(2) -> pushes lz of node2 to leaves 4,5, clears. r0>>1=3, ((6>>1)<<1)=6 ==r0 no. Then apply leaf5. Good. If root lz nonzero, push root before node2? At i=2 push root, then i=1 push node2; node2 lz may have been set? Actually root push propagates to node2 and node3, so node2 lz may become nonzero, then push node2. Good.

Let's write:

```
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    A = [0] + list(map(int, input().split()))
    size = 1
    log = 0
    while size < N:
        size <<= 1
        log += 1
    NEG = -10**18
    d = [NEG] * (2 * size)
    lz = [0] * size

    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        x = A[i]
        if not seen[x]:
            seen[x] = True
            cnt += 1
        d[size + i - 1] = cnt
    for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
        left = d[i << 1]
        right = d[i << 1 | 1]
        d[i] = left if left >= right else right

    def push(k):
        z = lz[k]
        if z:
            lc = k << 1
            d[lc] += z
            if lc < size:
                lz[lc] += z
            rc = lc | 1
            d[rc] += z
            if rc < size:
                lz[rc] += z
            lz[k] = 0

    def range_add(l, r, val):
        if l >= r:
            return
        l += size
        r += size
        l0 = l
        r0 = r
        for i in range(log, 0, -1):
            if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0:
                push(l0 >> i)
            if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0:
                push((r0 - 1) >> i)
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                d[l] += val
                if l < size:
                    lz[l] += val
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                d[r] += val
                if r < size:
                    lz[r] += val
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        l = l0
        r = r0
        for i in range(1, log + 1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                k = l >> i
                left = d[k << 1]
                right = d[k << 1 | 1]
                d[k] = left if left >= right else right
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                k = (r - 1) >> i
                left = d[k << 1]
                right = d[k << 1 | 1]
                d[k] = left if left >= right else right

    def range_max(l, r):
        if l >= r:
            return NEG
        l += size
        r += size
        for i in range(log, 0, -1):
            if ((l >> i) << i) != l:
                push(l >> i)
            if ((r >> i) << i) != r:
                push((r - 1) >> i)
        res = NEG
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                if d[l] > res:
                    res = d[l]
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                if d[r] > res:
                    res = d[r]
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        return res

    # suffix distinct
    suf = [0] * (N + 2)
    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    for i in range(N, 0, -1):
        x = A[i]
        if not seen[x]:
            seen[x] = True
            cnt += 1
        suf[i] = cnt

    last = [0] * (N + 1)
    ans = 0
    for j in range(2, N):  # j=2..N-1
        x = A[j]
        p = last[x]
        l = p - 1 if p > 0 else 0
        r = j - 1
        range_add(l, r, 1)
        best = range_max(0, r)
        cand = best + suf[j + 1]
        if cand > ans:
            ans = cand
        last[x] = j

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

We need a segment tree over d indices 0..N-1. Each leaf stores d_i. Each node stores:
- sum = sum of d in segment
- pref = maximum prefix sum within the segment, where prefix includes at least first element? For querying max prefix up to R, we can combine nodes left to right maintaining current sum and max. Standard.

For a node with left child L and right child R:
sum = L.sum + R.sum
pref = max(L.pref, L.sum + R.pref)
For leaf, sum = d_i, pref = d_i (prefix including the single element). But careful: We want max over p=0..R of prefix_sum_d[p] = sum_{0..p} d. This is exactly max prefix sum of array segment [0,R]. In a segment tree, if we query [0,R], combine nodes in order:
Initialize cur_sum=0, best=NEG.
For each segment node covering part of [0,R] in left-to-right order:
    best = max(best, cur_sum + node.pref)
    cur_sum += node.sum
Return best.
This works because node.pref is max prefix sum within that segment relative to segment start. Need ensure prefix can be empty? We want p from 0 to R inclusive, so prefix must include at least d_0? Actually prefix_sum_d[p] includes d_0..d_p. For the first segment, node.pref should be max over prefixes of that segment including at least first element of the whole array? Let's define node.pref as max over k=1..len of sum of first k elements in segment. Then for first segment, cur_sum=0, best=node.pref gives max prefix within first segment (including its first element). For subsequent segments, cur_sum is sum of previous segments, best=max(best, cur_sum + node.pref) gives prefix extending into this segment. This correctly includes d_0..d_p. This difference segment tree is much better. Let's formalize.

We maintain an array D[0..N-1] of differences such that for any position p (0-index left cut i=p+1), current value V_p = sum_{t=0}^p D[t] for p in active range. Initially V_p = f[p+1]. So D[0]=f[1], D[p]=f[p+1]-f[p] for p>=1.

When adding A[j] to middle, the set of p to increment is:
p = i-1, where i satisfies max(1, last_pos[A[j]]) <= i <= j-1.
So p_low = max(0, last_pos[A[j]] - 1)
p_high = j-2
We add +1 to V_p for p in [p_low, p_high]. In D, add +1 at p_low and -1 at p_high+1 = j-1.
Then query max V_p for p in [0, j-2] = prefix max of D up to j-2.

Need ensure p_high+1 = j-1 <= N-1? Since j<=N-1, j-1<=N-2, yes. So D index j-1 valid. If j=N-1, index N-2. Good. If j=2, index1. So D length N sufficient. What about p_low maybe p_high+1? p_low <= p_high, so distinct.

Now, segment tree over D supports:
- point_add(pos, delta)
- prefix_max(limit) where limit = p_high = j-2 (inclusive). Query range [0, limit] inclusive.

We can implement iterative segment tree for point update and range query with sum/pref. This is simpler and faster. Let's design.

Segment tree size power of two >= N. Leaves store D[i] for i=0..N-1, NEG for beyond. Each node stores sum and pref. We can store two arrays `seg_sum` and `seg_pref` length 2*size. For leaves:
sum[size+i] = D[i]
pref[size+i] = D[i]
For unused leaves, sum=0, pref=NEG? Need careful. If a node includes unused leaves with sum=0, pref=NEG, then combining should ignore them. For a segment that extends beyond N, we want it to act as identity for prefix max: sum=0, pref=-inf. Because if querying [0,R] with R<N, segment tree decomposition won't include nodes beyond R, but internal nodes that include unused leaves might be used if the query range covers up to a power-of-two boundary beyond N? Now, let's implement difference segment tree.

Segment tree node store sum and pref. We can use two lists `seg_sum` and `seg_pref` length 2*size. For leaves beyond N, set sum=0, pref=NEG. But for internal nodes, if both children have pref=NEG? Not used. Combining:
```
seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[2k] + seg_sum[2k+1]
seg_pref[k] = max(seg_pref[2k], seg_sum[2k] + seg_pref[2k+1])
```
If right child invalid with sum=0, pref=NEG, then left.pref dominates. If left child invalid? Could happen for nodes entirely beyond N; pref=NEG, sum=0. If a node has left invalid and right valid? Since leaves are filled from left, invalid leaves are at the end, so no node has left invalid and right valid. But if N not power of two, some internal node may have left valid, right invalid. Good. If both invalid, pref=NEG, sum=0. This works.

Point update:
```
def point_add(pos, delta):
    k = pos + size
    seg_sum[k] += delta
    seg_pref[k] += delta
    k >>= 1
    while k:
        seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[2k] + seg_sum[2k+1]
        seg_pref[k] = seg_pref[2k]
        cand = seg_sum[2k] + seg_pref[2k+1]
        if cand > seg_pref[k]: seg_pref[k] = cand
        k >>= 1
```
Need if leaf beyond N? We only update pos<=N-2, valid. Good.

```
    # Need combine left-to-right. Standard segment tree query with sum/pref.
    left_sum = 0
    left_pref = NEG? Actually we need accumulate nodes in order.
```
There are two ways:
1. Recursive/iterative collect nodes covering [0, limit+1) in left-to-right order, then combine.
2. Use segment tree query that maintains `res_sum`, `res_pref` for combined left part and `right_sum`, `right_pref` for right part. Since range starts at 0, simpler.

Because query range is always prefix [0, R], we can implement a specialized `prefix_max(R)` that traverses from root to collect nodes covering prefix. But iterative standard works.

Let's design iterative range query for sum/pref.

We need compute max prefix sum of D over positions 0..R. This is equivalent to querying segment [0, R+1). We can use a segment tree query that returns (sum, pref) for the concatenation of segments in order. Standard iterative query with two accumulators for left and right:

```
def range_query(l, r): # half-open [l,r)
    l += size; r += size
    # push? no lazy.
    left_sum = 0
    left_pref = NEG  # but for empty segment, pref should be -inf? We'll handle.
    right_sum = 0
    right_pref = NEG
    while l < r:
        if l & 1:
            # append node l to left result
            left_pref = max(left_pref, left_sum + seg_pref[l])
            left_sum += seg_sum[l]
            l += 1
        if r & 1:
            r -= 1
            # prepend node r to right result
            right_pref = max(seg_pref[r], seg_sum[r] + right_pref)
            right_sum = seg_sum[r] + right_sum
        l >>= 1; r >>= 1
    # combine left and right
    res_pref = max(left_pref, left_sum + right_pref)
    return res_pref
```
But for prefix query l=0, left_pref initially NEG. When first node appended, left_pref = max(NEG, 0 + node.pref) = node.pref, left_sum = node.sum. Good. If no nodes? R<0 not possible. For right accumulator, right_pref initially NEG; when prepending node, right_pref = max(node.pref, node.sum + right_pref). If right empty, right_pref=node.pref. Good. Combine left and right: res_pref = max(left_pref, left_sum + right_pref). This returns max prefix over concatenation. Need ensure if left empty, left_pref=NEG, left_sum=0, res_pref=right_pref. If right empty, right_pref=NEG, res_pref=left_pref. Good.

However, for prefix query starting at 0, we could simplify by traversing only right boundaries. But standard okay.

Since we only query [0, R+1), l=size always. The iterative query with l=size, r=size+R+1. It will collect nodes. Need no lazy. Complexity O(logN).

Let's test range_query for prefix [0,1) with size=4:
l=4,r=5.
while: l even, r odd -> r=4, prepend node4 to right: right_pref=pref4, right_sum=sum4. l=2,r=2 end. combine left_pref=NEG, left_sum=0, res=right_pref. Good.

Prefix [0,2): l=4,r=6.
while: l=4,r=6 -> l=2,r=3. l even, r odd -> r=2, prepend node2 to right: right_pref=pref2, right_sum=sum2. l=1,r=1 end. res=pref2. Good.

Prefix [0,3): l=4,r=7.
while: l=4,r=7 -> r odd -> r=6, prepend node6 (pos2) to right: right_pref=pref6, right_sum=sum6. l=2,r=3. l even, r odd -> r=2, prepend node2 (pos0,1) to right: right_pref = max(pref2, sum2 + pref6), right_sum=sum2+sum6. end. res=right_pref. This combines node2 then node6 in correct order because prepending node2 before node6. Good.

Prefix [0,4): l=4,r=8 -> l=2,r=4 -> l=1,r=2 -> r odd? r=2 even, l odd? l=1 odd -> append node1 to left? Let's simulate: l=4,r=8 -> l=2,r=4 -> l=1,r=2. l&1 => append node1 to left: left_pref=pref1, left_sum=sum1. l=2. r=2. end. res=left_pref. Good.

So range_query works.

Now, point update at pos: leaf sum += delta, pref += delta. For a leaf, sum=pref=D_i. If D_i changes by delta, both sum and pref change by delta. Correct.

Build:
```
seg_sum = [0]*(2*size)
seg_pref = [NEG]*(2*size)
for i in range(N):
    seg_sum[size+i] = D[i]
    seg_pref[size+i] = D[i]
for i in range(size-1,0,-1):
    seg_sum[i] = seg_sum[2*i] + seg_sum[2*i+1]
    seg_pref[i] = seg_pref[2*i]
    cand = seg_sum[2*i] + seg_pref[2*i+1]
    if cand > seg_pref[i]: seg_pref[i] = cand
```
For invalid leaves, sum=0, pref=NEG. If a node has left invalid and right invalid, sum=0, pref=max(NEG,0+NEG)=NEG. If left valid and right invalid, sum=left.sum, pref=left.pref. Good.

Let's implement `range_query(l, r)` generic for [l,r) maybe:
```
def query(l, r):
    l += size; r += size
    left_sum = 0
    left_pref = NEG
    right_sum = 0
    right_pref = NEG
    while l < r:
        if l & 1:
            # append l
            if left_pref == NEG:
                left_pref = seg_pref[l]
            else:
                cand = left_sum + seg_pref[l]
                if cand > left_pref: left_pref = cand
            left_sum += seg_sum[l]
            l += 1
        if r & 1:
            r -= 1
            # prepend r
            if right_pref == NEG:
                right_pref = seg_pref[r]
            else:
                cand = seg_sum[r] + right_pref
                if cand > right_pref: right_pref = cand
            right_sum = seg_sum[r] + right_sum
        l >>= 1; r >>= 1
    # combine
    res = left_pref
    cand = left_sum + right_pref
    if cand > res: res = cand
    return res
```
Need handle left_pref=NEG. Since left may be empty. If left empty, left_sum=0, left_pref=NEG. Combine with right: cand=0+right_pref. If right_pref=NEG too, res=NEG. But query nonempty. If left empty and right nonempty, res=right_pref. Good. If left nonempty and right empty, right_pref=NEG, cand=left_sum+NEG = very negative, res=left_pref. Good. But `left_sum + NEG` might be -1e18 + small, still >? NEG=-10**18, left_sum<=3e5, so cand ~ -999999999999700000, less than left_pref. Good.

For appending node to left:
If left_pref == NEG (empty), left_pref = seg_pref[l]. Else left_pref = max(left_pref, left_sum + seg_pref[l]). Then left_sum += seg_sum[l].
For prepending node to right:
If right_pref == NEG (empty), right_pref = seg_pref[r]. Else right_pref = max(seg_pref[r], seg_sum[r] + right_pref). Then right_sum = seg_sum[r] + right_sum.
This is standard.

Potential micro-optimizations:
- Bind `seg_sum`, `seg_pref`, `size` in closures; local variable access in nested functions is cell variable slower than local. We can define functions with default arguments:
```
def point_add(pos, delta, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size):
    ...
```
But `seg_sum` and `seg_pref` are mutated; default references okay. This makes them local variables inside function? Yes, default args are local variables. Similarly `prefix_max`. This can improve speed. However, `size` default local. Let's do that.

```
def point_add(pos, delta, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size):
    k = pos + size
    seg_sum[k] += delta
    seg_pref[k] += delta
    k >>= 1
    while k:
        left = k << 1
        right = left | 1
        seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[left] + seg_sum[right]
        v = seg_pref[left]
        cand = seg_sum[left] + seg_pref[right]
        if cand > v:
            v = cand
        seg_pref[k] = v
        k >>= 1
```
Need if leaf pref maybe NEG? We only update valid leaves, so no.

`prefix_max(limit, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size, NEG=NEG):`
```
def prefix_max(limit, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size, NEG=NEG):
    l = size
    r = size + limit + 1
    left_sum = 0
    left_pref = NEG
    right_sum = 0
    right_pref = NEG
    while l < r:
        if l & 1:
            if left_pref == NEG:
                left_pref = seg_pref[l]
            else:
                cand = left_sum + seg_pref[l]
                if cand > left_pref:
                    left_pref = cand
            left_sum += seg_sum[l]
            l += 1
        if r & 1:
            r -= 1
            if right_pref == NEG:
                right_pref = seg_pref[r]
            else:
                cand = seg_sum[r] + right_pref
                if cand > right_pref:
                    right_pref = cand
            right_sum = seg_sum[r] + right_sum
        l >>= 1
        r >>= 1
    res = left_pref
    cand = left_sum + right_pref
    if cand > res:
        res = cand
    return res
```
Since left_pref can be NEG and right_pref NEG, cand = 0 + NEG = NEG, res=NEG. But query nonempty. If left_pref == NEG and right_pref != NEG, res=right_pref. Good.

But note if left_pref is NEG and we append first node, we set left_pref=seg_pref[l]. If seg_pref[l] could be NEG? For a valid node covering only invalid leaves? Not selected. If selected node has valid leaves, pref not NEG. Good.

Could simplify prefix_max because l=size and query prefix; maybe use only right accumulator and then return right_pref? Let's see for prefix query, the selected nodes are all prepended to right; left accumulator remains empty until range covers whole tree? In standard query, l can become odd only if r is at size? Actually for prefix [0, limit+1) with limit+1 < size, l never odd as observed. If limit+1 == size, l odd selects root and right empty. So using both accumulators is fine. Could implement a simpler `prefix_max` by traversing from leaf r-1 up? But current okay.

Before final, let's do a thorough proof of difference method to catch any hidden off-by-one with D indexing.

Let's define 1-index A. Let f[i] = distinct A[1..i].
For a fixed right cut j (2..N-1), for each left cut i (1..j-1), define
S_j(i) = f[i] + distinct(A[i+1..j]).
We need max_i S_j(i) + distinct(A[j+1..N]).

We maintain an array B_p for p=0..N-1, where p=i-1. Initially B_p = f[p+1]. We want after processing A[1..j], for p=0..j-2, B_p = S_j(p+1). For p>=j-1, B_p remains f[p+1] (not used yet).

Represent B_p = prefix sum of D[0..p]. Initially D[p] = f[p+1]-f[p] (f[0]=0).

When adding A[j] (j>=2), for each p=0..j-2, B_p increases by 1 iff A[j] is not in A[p+2..j-1]. Let q be last occurrence of A[j] before j (0 if none). This condition is q <= p+1, i.e. p >= q-1. So p in [max(0,q-1), j-2]. Therefore in D, add +1 at max(0,q-1) and -1 at j-1. This leaves B_p for p<max unchanged, p in range +1, p>=j-1 unchanged (because +1 and -1 both included). Perfect.

Then query max_{p=0..j-2} B_p = prefix_max(j-2). Add suffix distinct.

Now, note for j=2, previous processed A[1]? We didn't process A[1] as middle, but D initial corresponds to j=1? For j=2, update A[2] as above. What about if A[2] appears in A[1]? q=1, p range [0,0], B_0 increases. Good. If A[2] not in A[1], q=0, p range [0,0]. Same. Good.

- sum[i] = sum of D in that range.
- pref[i] = max prefix sum within that range (relative to range start).
Point update at position p (1-index): for i=p; i<=N; i += i & -i:
    update sum[i] += delta
    recompute pref[i]? But pref[i] depends on children? In Fenwick, node i's range is not composed of two child ranges in a simple tree; updating a point affects O(logN) nodes, but to recompute pref[i] after updating one point, we need know the max prefix within the range. Let's maybe write final code now mentally:

```
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    A = [0] + list(map(int, input().split()))

    size = 1
    while size < N:
        size <<= 1

    NEG = -10**18
    seg_sum = [0] * (2 * size)
    seg_pref = [NEG] * (2 * size)

    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    prev = 0
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        x = A[i]
        if not seen[x]:
            seen[x] = True
            cnt += 1
        val = cnt - prev
        prev = cnt
        seg_sum[size + i - 1] = val
        seg_pref[size + i - 1] = val

    for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
        lch = i << 1
        rch = lch | 1
        seg_sum[i] = seg_sum[lch] + seg_sum[rch]
        v = seg_pref[lch]
        cand = seg_sum[lch] + seg_pref[rch]
        if cand > v:
            v = cand
        seg_pref[i] = v

    def point_add(pos, delta, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size):
        k = pos + size
        seg_sum[k] += delta
        seg_pref[k] += delta
        k >>= 1
        while k:
            lch = k << 1
            rch = lch | 1
            seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[lch] + seg_sum[rch]
            v = seg_pref[lch]
            cand = seg_sum[lch] + seg_pref[rch]
            if cand > v:
                v = cand
            seg_pref[k] = v
            k >>= 1

    def prefix_max(limit, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size, NEG=NEG):
        l = size
        r = size + limit + 1
        left_sum = 0
        left_pref = NEG
        right_sum = 0
        right_pref = NEG
        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                if left_pref == NEG:
                    left_pref = seg_pref[l]
                else:
                    cand = left_sum + seg_pref[l]
                    if cand > left_pref:
                        left_pref = cand
                left_sum += seg_sum[l]
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                if right_pref == NEG:
                    right_pref = seg_pref[r]
                else:
                    cand = seg_sum[r] + right_pref
                    if cand > right_pref:
                        right_pref = cand
                right_sum = seg_sum[r] + right_sum
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1
        res = left_pref
        cand = left_sum + right_pref
        if cand > res:
            res = cand
        return res

    suf = [0] * (N + 2)
    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    for i in range(N, 0, -1):
        x = A[i]
        if not seen[x]:
            seen[x] = True
            cnt += 1
        suf[i] = cnt

    last = [0] * (N + 1)
    last[A[1]] = 1

    ans = 0
    for j in range(2, N):
        x = A[j]
        q = last[x]
        l = q - 1 if q > 0 else 0
        R = j - 2
        point_add(l, 1)
        point_add(j - 1, -1)
        best = prefix_max(R)
        cand = best + suf[j + 1]
        if cand > ans:
            ans = cand
        last[x] = j

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `prefix_max` if `left_pref == NEG` and we append a node with pref negative but not
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    A = data
    A[0] = 0  # 1-indexed

    size = 1
    while size < N:
        size <<= 1

    NEG = -10**18
    seg_sum = [0] * (2 * size)
    seg_pref = [NEG] * (2 * size)

    # D[i] = f[i+1] - f[i], where f[i] is distinct count in A[1..i]
    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    prev = 0
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        x = A[i]
        if not seen[x]:
            seen[x] = True
            cnt += 1
        val = cnt - prev
        prev = cnt
        seg_sum[size + i - 1] = val
        seg_pref[size + i - 1] = val

    for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
        lch = i << 1
        rch = lch | 1
        seg_sum[i] = seg_sum[lch] + seg_sum[rch]
        v = seg_pref[lch]
        cand = seg_sum[lch] + seg_pref[rch]
        if cand > v:
            v = cand
        seg_pref[i] = v

    def point_add(pos, delta, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size):
        k = pos + size
        seg_sum[k] += delta
        seg_pref[k] += delta
        k >>= 1
        while k:
            lch = k << 1
            rch = lch | 1
            seg_sum[k] = seg_sum[lch] + seg_sum[rch]
            v = seg_pref[lch]
            cand = seg_sum[lch] + seg_pref[rch]
            if cand > v:
                v = cand
            seg_pref[k] = v
            k >>= 1

    def prefix_max(limit, seg_sum=seg_sum, seg_pref=seg_pref, size=size, NEG=NEG):
        l = size
        r = size + limit + 1
        left_sum = 0
        left_pref = NEG
        right_sum = 0
        right_pref = NEG

        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                if left_pref == NEG:
                    left_pref = seg_pref[l]
                else:
                    cand = left_sum + seg_pref[l]
                    if cand > left_pref:
                        left_pref = cand
                left_sum += seg_sum[l]
                l += 1

            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                if right_pref == NEG:
                    right_pref = seg_pref[r]
                else:
                    cand = seg_sum[r] + right_pref
                    if cand > right_pref:
                        right_pref = cand
                right_sum = seg_sum[r] + right_sum

            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1

        res = left_pref
        cand = left_sum + right_pref
        if cand > res:
            res = cand
        return res

    # suffix distinct counts
    suf = [0] * (N + 2)
    seen = [False] * (N + 1)
    cnt = 0
    for i in range(N, 0, -1):
        x = A[i]
        if not seen[x]:
            seen[x] = True
            cnt += 1
        suf[i] = cnt

    last = [0] * (N + 1)
    last[A[1]] = 1

    ans = 0

    for j in range(2, N):  # right cut j = 2..N-1
        x = A[j]
        q = last[x]
        l = q - 1 if q > 0 else 0
        R = j - 2

        # Add A[j] to the middle segment for left cuts p in [l, R]
        point_add(l, 1)
        point_add(j - 1, -1)

        best = prefix_max(R)
        cand = best + suf[j + 1]
        if cand > ans:
            ans = cand

        last[x] = j

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
